# Panel script

Draw-ready script for every chapter of *Harry Potter and the Philosopher’s Stone* in this book. Twenty-one chapters. First Lessons is its own chapter. Nicolas Flamel stays before the Mirror of Erised.

Image generation has not been started. Panels with no file say so. Existing files stay on the moment they already show, even if the page number changed.

A scene is one place and one stretch of time, and it takes at least one page of six panels. The first panels of a new place are the outside, the threshold, and what people were already doing, before the first new line of talk. Magic is a sequence: aim, the thin link on its way, the result, and a follow-through when someone is changed.

Counts: 21 chapters, 88 scenes, 165 pages, 990 panels.

## Chapters

- 1. The Boy Who Lived — 6 pages
- 2. Harry’s life with the Dursleys — 9 pages
- 3. The Keeper of the Keys — 5 pages
- 4. Diagon Alley — 9 pages
- 5. The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters — 8 pages
- 6. The Sorting Hat — 8 pages
- 7. First Lessons — 11 pages
- 8. The Potions Master — 6 pages
- 9. Flying class and the Seeker — 9 pages
- 10. The Midnight Duel — 10 pages
- 11. Hallowe’en — 11 pages
- 12. Quidditch — 6 pages
- 13. Nicolas Flamel — 7 pages
- 14. The Mirror of Erised — 5 pages
- 15. Norbert the Norwegian Ridgeback — 10 pages
- 16. The Forbidden Forest — 6 pages
- 17. They decide to protect the Stone — 9 pages
- 18. Through the Trapdoor — 11 pages
- 19. The Man with Two Faces — 6 pages
- 20. The hospital wing and the explanation — 6 pages
- 21. The House Cup — 7 pages

## Chapter 1 — The Boy Who Lived

### Scene — Privet Drive, one wet night

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 1 — The street before midnight

##### Panel 1

**Picture.** A wet suburban street seen from the corner, identical brick houses stepping away into fog. Street lamps burn warm yellow. Pavement shines. No one is walking.

**Faces.** The street is empty. Even the windows look asleep.

**Caption.** Privet Drive from the corner. Every lamp is still lit.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 2

**Picture.** The nearest house, number four, square and proud of its hedge. A milk bottle waits on the step. One window is warm. The door is shut and ordinary.

**Faces.** A closed house. Nothing welcomes anyone.

**Caption.** Number four, from the gate.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 3

**Picture.** From the garden side of the window: a floral sofa, a television, too many baby photographs, a lamp with a fringed shade. The room is whole and fussy. The sleepers are only shapes in it.

**Faces.** The room looks pleased with itself.

**Caption.** Through the glass, the room, not the people yet.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 4

**Picture.** Closer through the glass, still outside. Vernon is slumped in a brown suit. Petunia is asleep in a mint housedress. Baby Dudley sleeps in a frilly crib. None of them faces the window.

**Faces.** All three are slack, mouths open, dead to the street.

**Caption.** They were asleep before anyone arrived.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 5

**Picture.** A thin brown tabby with spectacle markings sits on the low garden wall, no collar, tail tucked. She watches the lamps. A moth bats the globe and she does not blink. She has been there all day.

**Faces.** The cat is perfectly still, ears forward, disgusted by the window and patient with the street.

**Caption.** A tabby has been on the wall for hours.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 6

**Picture.** At the dark end of Privet Drive, a very tall thin figure in a plum cloak and a pointed hat comes into the lamplight. A silver lighter-shaped device is closed in his hand. He has not raised it. The cat sees him first.

**Faces.** Dumbledore looks along the lamps, calm, not yet at the cat. The cat's eyes lock on him.

**Caption.** Someone tall steps into the far end of the street.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 2 — The street dies

##### Panel 7

**Picture.** Privet Drive just before midnight. Wet pavement, identical brick houses, fog at knee height. A thin tabby sits on a garden wall.

**Faces.** The cat stares down the street, ears forward, perfectly still.

**Caption.** Privet Drive, a few minutes before midnight.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p1-p1.jpg

##### Panel 8

**Picture.** Dumbledore stands at the left edge with the silver Deluminator in his hand, aimed at the nearest street lamp. A thin ribbon of warm yellow light runs from that lamp’s globe into the device. That lamp is going dark. Lamps behind him are out. Lamps farther ahead may still be lit. A thin brown tabby watches from the wall.

**Faces.** His eyes and his hand are on the lamp he is clicking. He does not look at the houses. The cat may watch.

**Caption.** Dumbledore puts out a street lamp.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p1-p2.jpg

##### Panel 9

**Picture.** Tight on the tabby’s face. The last lamp’s light is leaving the globe, a bright coin in each eye, then half gone. Wet brick and a few pale flowers.

**Faces.** Eyes wide and knowing. The cat stares toward where Dumbledore stands, out of frame.

**Caption.** The cat watches the last lamp go out.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p1-p3.jpg

##### Panel 10

**Picture.** Dumbledore has turned toward a low garden wall. A thin brown tabby with no collar sits there, still a cat. The street lamps behind them are already out.

**Faces.** He looks at the cat, recognizing her. The cat looks back, ears forward, perfectly still.

**Caption.** Dumbledore turns and notices the cat.

**Dialogue.**

- Dumbledore: I should have known that you would be here, Professor McGonagall.
- McGonagall: Good evening, Professor Dumbledore.

**Art.** art/c1-p1-p4.jpg

##### Panel 11

**Picture.** The wall is empty. McGonagall stands on the pavement where the cat was, emerald robes still settling, a wisp of tabby mist at her hem.

**Faces.** She looks up, stern and worried, mouth already open to argue.

**Caption.** The cat becomes Professor McGonagall.

**Dialogue.**

- McGonagall: Professor Dumbledore. I have been a cat on that wall since morning.

**Art.** art/c1-p1-p5.jpg

##### Panel 12

**Picture.** McGonagall and Dumbledore on the doorstep of number four. The dark front door sits between them. A milk bottle and trimmed hedges.

**Faces.** She looks at him, exasperated and afraid. He looks at the door, gentle and already decided.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- McGonagall: The Dursleys. Muggles of the worst sort.
- Dumbledore: And yet the boy must be left at number four, Privet Drive.

**Art.** art/c1-p1-p6.jpg

#### Page 3 — A lemon sweet

##### Panel 13

**Picture.** Dumbledore and McGonagall stand at the dark door of number four, where they have already been talking. He reaches into the plum cloak and brings out a small paper twist. The street behind them is already lamp-dark.

**Faces.** He looks mildly hopeful. She is still stern, eyes on the door, not on his hand.

**Caption.** On the step, he finds a paper twist.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 14

**Picture.** A single yellow lemon sweet sits in his long palm. He holds it out to her, between them, under the dead lamp.

**Faces.** His brows lift, a private kindness. She looks at the sweet as if it had no business on this night.

**Caption.** A lemon sweet, offered.

**Dialogue.**

- Dumbledore: A lemon sweet, Minerva?

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 15

**Picture.** McGonagall's hands stay in her sleeves. The sweet remains in his palm. Behind her, the warm window of the wrong family.

**Faces.** She is offended that the night has room for a sweet. He is not wounded by the refusal.

**Caption.** She will not take it.

**Dialogue.**

- McGonagall: No. Thank you, Professor Dumbledore.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 16

**Picture.** He puts the lemon sweet in his own mouth. A small sour wince, almost a smile, and then his face is grave again. The paper twist is empty.

**Faces.** The wince is tiny. The sadness was already there. She does not smile.

**Caption.** He eats it himself.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 17

**Picture.** He folds the paper away. His other hand is empty. He looks at the door, the decision already made. She looks through the window at the sleepers.

**Faces.** He is gentle and finished with the sweet. She is back to disgust.

**Caption.** The twist goes back into the cloak.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 18

**Picture.** The two of them are small against the house. The next thing the reader sees is the family inside. No motorcycle yet. The sweet is over.

**Faces.** McGonagall's mouth is tight. Dumbledore's eyes are sad, not joking.

**Caption.** They turn to the glass.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 4 — The wrong family

##### Panel 19

**Picture.** Through the front window: Vernon asleep in a brown suit, Petunia asleep in a mint housedress, baby Dudley in a frilly crib. Warm light, a floral sofa, too many baby photos.

**Faces.** All three are slack with sleep. None of them look toward the window.

**Caption.** Inside, the Dursleys sleep through the night.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p2-p1.jpg

##### Panel 20

**Picture.** McGonagall in profile outside the window, one hand half-raised, the warm room soft behind the glass.

**Faces.** Disgust and pity. She looks at Vernon, then at the blond baby.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- McGonagall: You cannot mean to leave a wizard child with these people. There is not a scrap of kindness in that sitting room.

**Art.** art/c1-p2-p2.jpg

##### Panel 21

**Picture.** Dumbledore from below, beard and hat against the black sky, one hand open. A few stars show where the cloud has torn.

**Faces.** Sad and kind, looking slightly down. He is not smiling.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Dumbledore: He will be famous, Minerva. Books will be written about him. Fame is a cruel weight for a child who cannot yet speak.

**Art.** art/c1-p2-p3.jpg

##### Panel 22

**Picture.** Most of the frame is sky over Privet Drive rooftops, chimneys, and aerials. A bulge hangs in the cloud. Dumbledore and McGonagall are small at the bottom.

**Faces.** McGonagall looks alarmed, eyes up. Dumbledore looks up with recognition, almost relief.

**Caption.** Something heavy comes down out of the cloud.

**Dialogue.**

- McGonagall: What in Merlin's name is that?
- Dumbledore: Hagrid. I asked him to bring the child.

**Art.** art/c1-p2-p4.jpg

##### Panel 23

**Picture.** A huge flying motorcycle drops through the fog above the hedge, headlamp cutting the dark, about to touch down. Hagrid cradles a green bundle.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks wrecked, eyes red, staring at the doorstep, not at the two wizards.

**Caption.** A flying motorcycle drops out of the sky.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p2-p5.jpg

##### Panel 24

**Picture.** The motorcycle has stopped on the drive. Hagrid holds the tiny green bundle. Dumbledore and McGonagall step in from either side. Crushed flowers, long headlamp shadows.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks at Dumbledore, begging and grieving. Dumbledore looks at the bundle. McGonagall looks at Hagrid, softened.

**Caption.** Hagrid lands with the baby.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Professor Dumbledore, sir. Professor McGonagall. I've got little Harry.

**Art.** art/c1-p2-p6.jpg

#### Page 5 — The night Voldemort fell

##### Panel 25

**Picture.** Close on Hagrid’s face and the top of the baby’s black hair. Headlamp glow, an out-of-focus hedge.

**Faces.** He looks down at Harry, mouth crumpled, trying to speak. Eyes shining.

**Caption.** Hagrid's voice breaks.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: I got him out of the house. I got him out, but James and Lily...

**Art.** art/c1-p3-p1.jpg

##### Panel 26

**Picture.** A memory with jagged edges: a stone cottage, roof blown open, a spear of green light in the doorway. James face-down, glasses aside. Lily collapsed by the crib, one arm reaching.

**Faces.** No living faces to read. The reaching hand is the feeling of the picture.

**Caption.** A flash of the ruined cottage and a green light.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p3-p2.jpg

##### Panel 27

**Picture.** Hagrid holds Harry. McGonagall is close, one hand lifted and stopping. Dumbledore stands behind them, hat brim low. The motorcycle ticks as it cools.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks wrecked. McGonagall looks at the baby, eyes wet, mouth tight. Dumbledore looks into the dark.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: They're dead. Both of them. James and Lily Potter.

**Art.** art/c1-p3-p3.jpg

##### Panel 28

**Picture.** Hagrid hunches over the bright green blanket. Dumbledore’s hand enters, palm down, a calming gesture. Fog around their knees.

**Faces.** Hagrid flinches, eyes squeezed, looking at the hand. Dumbledore’s visible eye is steady and sad.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: It was You-Know-Who. He killed them. And he tried for the baby an' all.
- Dumbledore: Say the name, Hagrid. Voldemort failed.

**Art.** art/c1-p3-p4.jpg

##### Panel 29

**Picture.** The baby’s face fills the panel, looking straight out, so his anatomical right brow is on the viewer’s left. A fresh lightning cut runs from that brow up into the black tuft. It is not on the left brow, the chin, or the cheek. A purple sleeve, a huge thumb, and emerald cloth frame the green blanket. Fog and the dark door are only edges.

**Faces.** Baby Harry looks straight out, calm, not crying. The scar is the fresh cut on his right brow. Dumbledore looks at that brow. Hagrid looks at the baby’s eyes.

**Caption.** The blanket is folded back. The scar is on his right brow.

**Dialogue.**

- Dumbledore: See the scar. A lightning cut. That is where the curse struck.

**Art.** art/c1-p3-p5.jpg

##### Panel 30

**Picture.** McGonagall in profile, hat against her chest. Hagrid hides his face in one hand. Harry is awake between them. The doorstep enters the bottom of the frame.

**Faces.** McGonagall looks at Harry, awe under the sternness. Hagrid’s eyes are shut. Harry looks into the dark.

**Caption.** Hagrid sobs.

**Dialogue.**

- McGonagall: So this is the boy who lived.
- Hagrid: Aye. And he's only a baby.

**Art.** art/c1-p3-p6.jpg

#### Page 6 — The letter and the lights

##### Panel 31

**Picture.** Hagrid kneels so his face meets Dumbledore’s chest, one hand open toward the house. Dumbledore holds the baby. McGonagall stands aside, arms folded. A milk bottle, dark windows.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks up, pleading. Dumbledore looks down with love and refusal. McGonagall looks at the door, angry that she agrees.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Don't leave him here, sir. Please. Anywhere but with them.

**Art.** art/c1-p4-p1.jpg

##### Panel 32

**Picture.** Dumbledore stands straight, the baby in one arm, his other hand on Hagrid’s shoulder. Fog, a dark motorcycle, the house looming.

**Faces.** Dumbledore looks gentle and final. Hagrid looks at the baby, giving up. Harry’s eyes are on the beard.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Dumbledore: He must grow up away from our world, Hagrid. Away from the stories, until he is old enough to bear them.

**Art.** art/c1-p4-p2.jpg

##### Panel 33

**Picture.** Close on a hand sliding a thick sealed envelope into the green blanket, beside the baby’s small fist. A wax seal catches the light.

**Faces.** Harry does not react. The hand is careful, almost reluctant.

**Caption.** A letter is tucked into the blankets.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p4-p3.jpg

##### Panel 34

**Picture.** From the doormat: Harry is small against the big black door, the letter peeking from the blanket. Three pairs of feet stand back.

**Faces.** Harry looks up, calm, toward the people leaving. Only their feet are in the frame.

**Caption.** Harry is set on the doorstep, eyes open.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p4-p4.jpg

##### Panel 35

**Picture.** High over the street. Harry is a tiny green mark on the step. The motorcycle lifts, the tabby glances back from a wall, and Dumbledore walks into the dark.

**Faces.** Hagrid’s back is all we see. The cat’s eyes flash once. Dumbledore’s face is hidden by the hat brim.

**Caption.** They leave in three directions.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p4-p5.jpg

##### Panel 36

**Picture.** At the far corner the same silver Deluminator is in his hand. Thin ribbons of warm yellow run from the device up into the street lamps, and the lamps flare on. Harry lies alone in that light on the step. The door has not opened.

**Faces.** Harry looks toward the street, eyes open, not crying. Dumbledore is already leaving at the edge. The light is warm and the picture is lonely.

**Caption.** The light returns. The lamps flare on. The baby lies alone.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c1-p4-p6.jpg

## Chapter 2 — Harry’s life with the Dursleys

### Scene — The house on Dudley's birthday

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 1 — Morning on Privet Drive

##### Panel 1

**Picture.** The same brick house, now morning. A neat lawn, the same hedge, a car in the drive. Nothing looks magical. Neighbors' houses match it down to the curtains.

**Faces.** A proud, ordinary house.

**Caption.** Number four in ordinary daylight.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 2

**Picture.** The door of number four, brass numbers, a mail slot, a doormat. Petunia's shadow moves behind the frosted glass, busy, not opening the door.

**Faces.** The house is already in a rush that does not include a small boy.

**Caption.** The front door, from the path.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 3

**Picture.** Inside the hall: floral carpet, a row of Dudley's framed photos climbing the stairs, Vernon's coat on a hook. The cupboard door under the stairs is shut. A bolt is on the outside.

**Faces.** The hall is crowded with one child's life and a locked door.

**Caption.** The hall, before the cupboard opens.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 4

**Picture.** Through the kitchen doorway, Dudley sits in a striped shirt among a mountain of bright parcels, mouth open, mid-count, delighted with himself. Vernon beams over a newspaper. The table is a feast.

**Faces.** Dudley is greedy and happy. Vernon is proud. Neither looks toward the hall.

**Caption.** Dudley was already counting in the kitchen.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 5

**Picture.** At the end of the table, away from the parcels, one plate holds a single slice of toast. Petunia sets it down without looking at it, then turns back to Dudley's pile.

**Faces.** She is brisk and blank. The toast is an afterthought.

**Caption.** Petunia sets one slice of toast.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 6

**Picture.** Back in the hall, close on the cupboard under the stairs. The bolt. A thin line of dark inside. A small movement, not yet a hand. The kitchen noise is far away.

**Faces.** The door looks like a cupboard. It is a bedroom.

**Caption.** The cupboard door, still shut.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 2 — The cupboard

##### Panel 7

**Picture.** A small hand pushes the cupboard door from inside. Dusty light cuts across cobwebs, a thin mattress, and the sloped underside of the stairs.

**Faces.** Sleepy and wary. He looks out into the hall light as if checking whether it is safe.

**Caption.** Harry wakes in the cupboard under the stairs.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c2-p1-p1.jpg

##### Panel 8

**Picture.** Harry sits under the stairs, knees up, the ceiling too low to stand. The open door frames a polished banister and a bright kitchen.

**Faces.** He looks at his own knees, resigned. This is an ordinary morning.

**Caption.** He sits up under the stairs.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c2-p1-p2.jpg

##### Panel 9

**Picture.** The sitting room is buried in wrapping paper. Dudley stands among the presents. Harry is a thin figure in the doorway.

**Faces.** Dudley looks at the next present, delighted. Vernon and Petunia look at Dudley. Harry looks at the pile, blank.

**Caption.** Dudley counts a mountain of presents.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Thirty-six! Count them, Dad, thirty-six presents!
- Vernon: And a fine boy deserves every one.

**Art.** art/c2-p1-p3.jpg

##### Panel 10

**Picture.** The kitchen table is split. One dry slice of toast on the left. Eggs, bacon, and a stack of toast on the right.

**Faces.** Harry looks at his toast. Dudley checks that Harry's plate is smaller. Petunia watches Dudley's plate. Vernon hides in the paper.

**Caption.** Toast for Harry. A feast for Dudley.

**Dialogue.**

- Petunia: Eat your toast. And don't you stare at Dudley's plate.

**Art.** art/c2-p1-p4.jpg

##### Panel 11

**Picture.** Vernon lowers the paper just enough to show his eyes and mustache. One thick finger points at Harry. The cupboard door is ajar down the hall.

**Faces.** Vernon glares, warning. Harry looks back, cautious, mouth closed.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: No questions. We do not speak of that rubbish in this house, boy.

**Art.** art/c2-p1-p5.jpg

##### Panel 12

**Picture.** A framed photo of Vernon, Petunia, and Dudley hangs on floral wallpaper. Harry stands outside the frame, looking up at the place where he is not.

**Faces.** The photo-family looks delighted. Harry looks quiet and hurt.

**Caption.** The family photo has no Harry in it.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c2-p1-p6.jpg

### Scene — The zoo, one snake

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 3 — The zoo, from the gate

##### Panel 13

**Picture.** A municipal zoo entrance, balloons, a sign, families queueing. Vernon marches in a brown suit. Petunia holds Dudley's shoulder. Harry trails in Dudley's old gray clothes, a camera he is not allowed to touch.

**Faces.** Dudley is already bored and loud. Harry is small and quiet. Vernon looks as if the outing is a duty.

**Caption.** The zoo gates, a bright cold day.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 14

**Picture.** A low brick reptile house, steamed windows, a painted snake on a board. Other families drift toward the penguins. Dudley points at this door and pulls.

**Faces.** Dudley wants something to bang. Harry looks at the door, curious, not yet inside.

**Caption.** The reptile house from the path.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 15

**Picture.** The doorway. Outside is gray daylight. Inside is green glass, wet air, and a hiss of heaters. The room opens as one long dim hall of tanks.

**Faces.** Harry's glasses fog. Dudley wrinkles his nose. Petunia already dislikes the smell.

**Caption.** They cross into the heat.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 16

**Picture.** The hall of enclosures. Visitors drift. A child taps a lizard tank and is pulled away. At the far end, one large tank is darker than the others.

**Faces.** Strangers are casual. Harry is the one who looks properly.

**Caption.** Tanks, and people already tapping glass.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 17

**Picture.** The far tank. One large green-brown boa with darker saddles fills the floor of the enclosure, longer than a child is tall, thick, one pale gold eye half open. It does not perform. The sign is a little dirty.

**Faces.** The snake is bored with the public. It has not looked at Harry yet.

**Caption.** One boa, already there, ignoring everyone.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 18

**Picture.** Dudley reaches the tank, both hands rising, mouth open, about to hit the glass. Harry stands a step back, looking at the gold eye. Vernon is a mustache in the background, checking his watch.

**Faces.** Dudley is impatient. Harry is quiet and already on the snake's side. The snake has not moved.

**Caption.** Dudley arrives at the glass. He has not banged yet.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 4 — The zoo

##### Panel 19

**Picture.** The reptile house. Dudley presses both hands to the glass. Behind it is one large green-brown boa with darker saddles, thick enough to fill the enclosure floor, longer than Dudley is tall, the same pale gold eye. Harry stands a step behind. Vernon, large and beefy, big black mustache, brown coat, is a soft shape down the hall with Petunia.

**Faces.** Dudley wants a trick. The snake looks past him. Harry looks at the snake, curious and gentle.

**Caption.** Dudley bangs on the snake's glass.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Make it move! It's a boring snake!

**Art.** art/c2-p2-p1.jpg

##### Panel 20

**Picture.** Through the glass, the same boa's pale gold eye is aimed past Dudley toward Harry. Same broad head, same darker saddles, not a new snake in close-up. Dudley is a blur at the edge.

**Faces.** The snake looks only at Harry. Dudley does not understand he has been dismissed. Harry looks back, startled and pleased.

**Caption.** The snake ignores Dudley and looks at Harry.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c2-p2-p2.jpg

##### Panel 21

**Picture.** Harry is close to the glass, one hand spread on the pane. The same green-brown boa, still longer than he is tall, has slid nearer, tongue almost touching the spot opposite his fingers.

**Faces.** Harry looks into the snake's eye, earnest. The snake looks back, steady. No fear on either side.

**Caption.** The snake answers by moving.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: Sorry about him. You get tired of being stuck behind glass, don't you.

**Art.** art/c2-p2-p3.jpg

##### Panel 22

**Picture.** Dudley shoves Harry. The tank's glass is gone, and the same thick green-brown boa pours over the rim. It is not a bigger snake and not a smaller one.

**Faces.** Dudley looks confused. Harry looks at the empty frame, shocked. The snake looks at the open hall.

**Caption.** Dudley shoves Harry. The glass is gone.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Mum! The glass has gone!

**Art.** art/c2-p2-p4.jpg

##### Panel 23

**Picture.** Dudley topples into the tank. The same boa, darker saddles and the same pale gold eye, slides across the tile past Harry, unhurried, toward daylight. Petunia watches in horror.

**Faces.** Dudley looks up, furious and scared. Harry looks down at the snake, almost kind. The snake does not look back.

**Caption.** Dudley falls in. The snake slides past.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Get me out! Harry did it!

**Art.** art/c2-p2-p5.jpg

##### Panel 24

**Picture.** The zoo exit. Vernon has Harry by the windcheater and hauls him through the crowd. Petunia fusses over a wet Dudley.

**Faces.** Vernon glares down, blaming Harry. Harry looks ahead, jaw tight. Strangers look uneasy.

**Caption.** Vernon drags Harry out through the crowd.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: You did that on purpose. You and that snake. Walk.

**Art.** art/c2-p2-p6.jpg

### Scene — The letters

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 5 — The kitchen, before the owl

##### Panel 25

**Picture.** The brick house from the street, morning, chimney smoke. A tawny owl is a speck over the roofs, not yet at the window. The neighborhood looks normal.

**Faces.** An ordinary street, if you miss the owl.

**Caption.** Number four again, another morning.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 26

**Picture.** Through the window: the breakfast table, eggs, Vernon's paper, Dudley's large plate, Harry's small one. The window is shut. The owl is closer, a blur above the hedge.

**Faces.** Inside, they are eating. Nobody has looked up.

**Caption.** The kitchen window from the garden.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 27

**Picture.** The kitchen as a whole. Toast, eggs, a teapot, Dudley's empty wrappers from yesterday still in a corner. Harry sits at the edge of the table in a shirt too big at the neck. The mail slot is quiet in the hall beyond.

**Faces.** Vernon is buried in the paper. Petunia is serving Dudley. Harry is hungry and careful.

**Caption.** They cross nothing. We are already in the smell of eggs.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 28

**Picture.** Dudley waves a fork, telling a story nobody needs. Crumbs on his chin. Harry watches the window, not the feast. His plate is eggs and nothing extra.

**Faces.** Dudley is loud. Harry is elsewhere. Petunia watches Dudley, not Harry.

**Caption.** Dudley talks with his mouth full. Harry does not.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 29

**Picture.** Vernon snaps the newspaper flat. A headline about ordinary news. His mustache is calm. This is the last ordinary second.

**Faces.** He looks satisfied. He has not gone white yet.

**Caption.** Vernon turns a page. The house is pleased with itself.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 30

**Picture.** A wing-shadow slides over the tablecloth and the eggs. Harry's eyes go up. Dudley's fork stops halfway. The owl is at the glass and has not dropped the letter.

**Faces.** Harry is startled and hopeful. Dudley is confused. Vernon has not looked up from the paper.

**Caption.** A shadow crosses the eggs.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 6 — The letters

##### Panel 31

**Picture.** Breakfast. A tawny owl is just leaving the window. A thick envelope has landed by the eggs. Vernon's fork has stopped.

**Faces.** Vernon stares at the owl, outraged. Petunia stares at the letter, afraid. Dudley is delighted. Harry looks hungry with hope.

**Caption.** An owl drops a letter onto the eggs.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Dad, an owl! A real owl in the kitchen!

**Art.** art/c2-p3-p1.jpg

##### Panel 32

**Picture.** The letter is crushed in Vernon's fist. Harry's hand is still open where it was. Vernon's face has lost its purple.

**Faces.** Vernon looks at the address, afraid. Harry looks at the letter, desperate.

**Caption.** Vernon snatches the letter and goes white.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: Give me that. You will not read it.
- Harry: It's addressed to me. To the cupboard under the stairs.

**Art.** art/c2-p3-p2.jpg

##### Panel 33

**Picture.** Petunia bends to Vernon's ear. The letter is open, a crest at the top. The kitchen has gone still.

**Faces.** Petunia sees a memory. Vernon looks up at her, lost. Harry sees a crack in her for the first time.

**Caption.** Petunia reads over his shoulder.

**Dialogue.**

- Petunia: Lily. It's from her sort. I knew this day would come.

**Art.** art/c2-p3-p3.jpg

##### Panel 34

**Picture.** The next day. Vernon kneels at the front door, hammer raised, nails in his teeth. Three thick letters push through the slot. Harry watches from the stairs.

**Faces.** Vernon hates the slot. Harry looks at the letters, longing. Petunia looks at Harry, guilty and angry. Dudley is starting to be scared.

**Caption.** The next day, Vernon nails the mail slot shut.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: No more letters. Not through this door. Not to any cupboard in my house.

**Art.** art/c2-p3-p4.jpg

##### Panel 35

**Picture.** Envelopes blast out of the fireplace, dozens, then hundreds. Dudley is backed into a corner. Harry stands in the paper storm, reaching.

**Faces.** Dudley looks up, terrified. Harry looks at a letter just out of reach, almost laughing. Vernon looks furious and small.

**Caption.** Letters pour down the chimney.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Make them stop! They're coming down the chimney!

**Art.** art/c2-p3-p5.jpg

##### Panel 36

**Picture.** Vernon hammers a plank across the fireplace. Letters bulge behind the wood. Harry holds one letter against his stomach. Dudley cries into Petunia's apron.

**Faces.** Vernon looks at Harry, wild. Harry looks back, defiant. Petunia looks at Vernon as if she does not know him.

**Caption.** Vernon boards up the fireplace.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: I will not have magic in this house. Do you hear me, boy?

**Art.** art/c2-p3-p6.jpg

**Faces.** The house looks like it is under siege by paper.

**Caption.** Number four with the windows boarded.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 38

**Picture.** Vernon in a knit vest over his shirt, hair wet, heaves a bag into the boot as if the bag had insulted him. Petunia holds a coat over her hair. Dudley is a round shape under a jacket, already climbing in.

**Faces.** Vernon is purple with effort and fury. Petunia is pinched. Dudley is cross.

##### Panel 39

##### Panel 40

**Caption.** The road becomes a sea road.

##### Panel 42

**Picture.** A shack on a rock, waves at the walls, one weak bulb maybe unlit. The car crawls the last of the causeway. They have not gone in. No one has spoken in these pictures.

**Caption.** The rock, closer, the hut still a shut box.

### Scene — The hut on the rock

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 8 — The hut

##### Panel 43

**Picture.** Inside the car, rain smears the windows. Vernon drives. Dudley takes the seat. Harry is wedged against the door. A letter rides the storm on the glass.

**Faces.** Vernon glares at the road, hunted. Petunia looks at the letter, pale. Dudley looks at his chocolate. Harry looks at the letter, hopeful.

**Caption.** The car races into a storm.

- Petunia: There's another one. Stuck to the glass.
- Vernon: Don't look at it. Drive.

##### Panel 44

**Picture.** A tiny hut on a rock in black water. The car is parked at a mad angle. A wave explodes against the wall. Four figures run for the door.

**Caption.** A shack on a rock. Waves hit the walls.

**Dialogue.** None.

##### Panel 45

**Picture.** They have arrived. The shack door, swollen wood, a simple latch, spray on the step. Vernon shoulders it. The sea is white behind him. This is the outside of the room they are about to enter.

**Caption.** The hut door from the wind.

##### Panel 46

**Picture.** The interior, whole: one room, a cot, a stove, a table, a hanging bulb unlit at first, then a match. Damp walls. The sea is loud and the room is smaller than Vernon's confidence.

**Caption.** They cross into one bare room.

##### Panel 47

**Picture.** Dudley is already on the cot, shoes muddy, eating something he saved. Petunia spreads a handkerchief to sit on, and fails. Harry is on the floor near the wall, knees up.

**Caption.** Dudley takes the only cot.

**Dialogue.** None.

##### Panel 48

**Picture.** Vernon stands at the shut door, mustache wet, listening to the wind as if it were an intruder. A rifle leans in the corner, still straight, not in his hands. He has not boasted yet. He has not grabbed it.

**Caption.** Vernon watches the door. The rifle leans in the corner.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 9 — Inside the hut

##### Panel 49

**Picture.** Harry against the damp wall, a watchless wrist, lips moving in a count he has not said aloud. Midnight is close. A letter could still be in the storm. He does not look at Vernon.

**Faces.** He is hopeful and tired. Nobody is looking at him.

**Caption.** Harry counts nothing yet. He listens.

**Dialogue.** None.

##### Panel 50

**Picture.** Wide again: the cot, the rifle, the stove, four people, and the door. No knock yet. The next sound belongs to the old page. Here, there is only weather.

**Caption.** The room holds its breath.

##### Panel 51

**Picture.** Inside the hut, Vernon spreads his arms. Water drips from the ceiling into a pan. The family is miserable around him.

- Vernon: Let the owls try. Nobody will find us on this rock.

**Picture.** Harry sits alone in the corner and counts on his fingers. The bulb flickers. Behind him the Dursleys sleep in a heap.

- Harry: Ten. Nine. Happy birthday. Nobody has to know.

**Picture.** The storm holds still. A shadow too big to be an ordinary man crosses the salt-crusted window. The bulb stops flickering.

## Chapter 3 — The Keeper of the Keys

### Scene — The hut, night into dawn

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 1 — The door

##### Panel 1

**Picture.** The shack door is off its hinges and falling inward. Hagrid fills the doorway, so large the night sea is only a strip around his shoulders. The bulb swings. Spray blows in.

**Faces.** Hagrid's fierceness cracks when he finds Harry. Vernon looks up, mustache shaking. Harry looks up, stunned.

**Caption.** The door blasts in. Hagrid fills the frame.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Sorry about the door.

**Art.** art/c3-p1-p1.jpg

##### Panel 2

**Picture.** Hagrid kneels and hugs Harry off his feet. Only sneakers and one arm show. A squashed cake tin is still in Hagrid's other hand. The Dursleys press against the far wall.

**Faces.** Hagrid's eyes are shut, crying and smiling. Harry's visible eye is wide, looking sideways, not sure he is allowed to be held.

**Caption.** Hagrid hugs Harry off his feet.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Happy birthday, Harry.

**Art.** art/c3-p1-p2.jpg

##### Panel 3

**Picture.** The rifle barrel is a knot. Vernon's hands are still on the stock. Hagrid uses two fingers.

**Faces.** Vernon stares at the knot, belief breaking. Hagrid looks mildly annoyed. Harry watches with a startled almost-smile.

**Caption.** Hagrid bends the rifle into a knot.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: I demand that you leave this instant!
- Hagrid: Mind the rifle, Mr Dursley. It bends.

**Art.** art/c3-p1-p3.jpg

##### Panel 4

**Picture.** A thick damp envelope with a wax seal sits on Hagrid's open palm, bigger than Harry's hand. The cake tin is on the table. The knotted rifle lies on the floor.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks encouraging. Harry looks hungry and afraid to hope. Vernon looks at the letter with hate.

**Caption.** Hagrid holds out the soggy letter.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: This letter's yours. Hogwarts has been trying to reach you for days.

**Art.** art/c3-p1-p4.jpg

##### Panel 5

**Picture.** Over Harry's shoulder. The letter is open, a crest at the top. His finger rests under a line. The bulb lights the parchment. Water drips into a pan.

**Faces.** Harry's brow is tight with confusion and want. Hagrid watches his face, waiting for the smile.

**Caption.** Harry reads the word Hogwarts.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry.

**Art.** art/c3-p1-p5.jpg

##### Panel 6

**Picture.** Harry lowers the letter and looks up. He holds it against his chest. Hagrid is a warm blur behind him.

**Faces.** Harry looks lost. His eyes are wet from the weather and from being seen.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: A wizard? I don't even know what that means.

**Art.** art/c3-p1-p6.jpg

#### Page 2 — Denial

##### Panel 7

**Picture.** Vernon jabs a finger up at Hagrid's chest. Harry keeps the letter behind him. Dudley hides by Petunia, chocolate on his chin.

**Faces.** Vernon shouts. Hagrid looks down, insulted. Harry is afraid the letter will be taken. Dudley stares at the boots.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: There is no such thing as magic. And you are not going to any school of freaks.

**Art.** art/c3-p2-p1.jpg

##### Panel 8

**Picture.** Hagrid draws himself up under the low ceiling and points the pink umbrella, scolding, not firing it. The bulb swings away from his head.

**Faces.** Hagrid is genuinely offended and glances down to be sure Harry hears. Vernon looks at the umbrella, nerves showing.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Watch your mouth. Hogwarts is the finest school of witchcraft in the world, and Harry's got a place in it.

**Art.** art/c3-p2-p2.jpg

##### Panel 9

**Picture.** Dudley's hands are in the cake tin, frosting on his fingers. The lid is on the floor. Hagrid's back is to the table.

**Faces.** Dudley looks at Harry, mocking. Harry looks hurt. Petunia looks away and does not stop her son.

**Caption.** Dudley laughs and steals the cake.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Ha! His cake's squashed. I'm having it.

**Art.** art/c3-p2-p3.jpg

##### Panel 10

**Picture.** The same hut, a second later. Vernon is purple in the face at the table, the knotted rifle in his fist, brown suit, little neck, big black mustache. He is not casting anything. Harry holds the soggy letter in Dudley’s too-big gray shirt. Hagrid’s huge hand has found the pink umbrella in the moleskin coat and has not raised it.

**Faces.** Vernon is furious and still denying. Harry is small and watchful. Hagrid’s kindness is thinning.

**Caption.** Hagrid finds the umbrella.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 11

**Picture.** Hagrid turns from the cake tin. The pink umbrella comes up. Dudley, blond and heavy, frosting on his mouth, is still laughing with his back half turned. No spark yet. Rain in the doorway. Petunia’s pinned blonde hair, long neck, mint or night clothes, hands not yet in her hair.

**Faces.** Hagrid’s beetle-black eyes lock on Dudley. Dudley is delighted with himself. Harry’s mouth is not smiling yet.

**Caption.** The umbrella comes up.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 12

**Picture.** Camera at the table height. The umbrella tip points at the seat of Dudley’s trousers. Hagrid’s eyes stay on that spot, mouth set. The umbrella is his wand. Nothing has left the tip. Cake is still in Dudley’s hands.

**Faces.** Hagrid is aimed and finished warning. Dudley has not noticed the aim. Harry watches the tip.

**Caption.** He aims. No spark yet.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: I said that's enough.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 3 — The umbrella and the tail

##### Panel 13

**Picture.** Inside the hut, same rain at the broken door, same lamplight. Hagrid’s pink umbrella is aimed at the seat of Dudley’s trousers. A thin bright thread leaves the umbrella tip and crosses the room to that cloth. It does not light every face and it does not fill the hut. Cake crumbs hang in the air. Dudley still has cake in his mouth. The tail is not there yet.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks annoyed, not cruel, eyes on the trousers. Dudley’s laugh is dying as he sees the thread. Harry flinches, and wonder breaks through. Petunia has not understood.

**Caption.** A thin spark leaves the umbrella.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: That's enough out of you.

**Art.** art/c3-p2-p4.jpg

##### Panel 14

**Picture.** Dudley sprints for the broken door, both hands on his backside. A curly pink tail sticks out through his trousers. Cake is still in his mouth.

**Faces.** Dudley howls. Petunia's mind breaks. Harry's real smile starts. Hagrid looks satisfied and a bit guilty.

**Caption.** A curly tail pokes through Dudley's trousers.

**Dialogue.**

- Dudley: Mum! There's a tail! I've got a tail!

**Art.** art/c3-p2-p5.jpg

##### Panel 15

**Picture.** The hut is in uproar. Petunia's hands are in her hair. Harry covers a small smile with the letter. Vernon sits down hard. Dudley runs into the rain.

**Faces.** Petunia shrieks. Harry smiles despite himself. Hagrid softens when he sees it. Vernon looks defeated.

**Caption.** Petunia shrieks. Harry almost smiles.

**Dialogue.**

- Petunia: What have you done to my Dudley?

**Art.** art/c3-p2-p6.jpg

##### Panel 16

**Picture.** The doorway of the hut, rain. Dudley sprints onto the rock with the curly pink tail sticking through his trousers and cake still in his mouth. Petunia is in the door behind him, hands in her pinned hair. Harry is a small shape in the lamplight with the letter over his mouth.

**Faces.** Dudley howls into the weather. Petunia’s mind has broken. Harry’s real smile is starting. Hagrid looks satisfied and a bit guilty.

**Caption.** He runs into the rain with the tail.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 17

**Picture.** Back inside, the umbrella lowered. The thin thread is gone. Dudley is a wet shape outside. Vernon has sat down hard, the knotted rifle across his knees. Harry covers the smile with the letter. The hut is the same room.

**Faces.** Harry smiles despite himself. Hagrid softens when he sees it. Vernon looks defeated. Petunia is still in the door, shrieking.

**Caption.** The spark is over. The tail is not.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 18

**Picture.** Wide on the hut from just inside. One room, one lamp, rain. The cake tin is empty. The umbrella hangs at Hagrid’s side. Nobody is glowing. The next talk is the story of Harry’s parents, still in this room.

**Faces.** The room is quieter and worse. Harry looks at Hagrid, ready to hear it. Hagrid looks sorry for the fun he just had.

**Caption.** The hut settles. The story has not started.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 4 — The story

##### Panel 19

**Picture.** Hagrid sits on the floor so he can speak quietly. The cake tin is between them. A blanket is around Harry's shoulders. He holds a mug too big for him. The Dursleys huddle far behind.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks gentle and grave. Harry looks guarded, because good news in this house usually is not.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: That story they told you, about a car crash. It's a lie, Harry.

**Art.** art/c3-p3-p1.jpg

##### Panel 20

**Picture.** Harry's hands tighten on the mug. Tea jumps. Hagrid's big hands are open. The Dursleys are out of the frame.

**Faces.** Hagrid does not look away. Shock replaces the car-crash story on Harry's face. No tears yet.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Your mum and dad were a witch and a wizard. And they were murdered.

**Art.** art/c3-p3-p2.jpg

##### Panel 21

**Picture.** Hagrid leans in. The bulb seems dimmer. His hand half-covers his mouth after the name.

**Faces.** Hagrid flinches, afraid of the name, then looks back to be brave. Harry catches the fear before the meaning.

**Caption.** Hagrid flinches at the name.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: It was Voldemort. Most folk won't say it. They call him You-Know-Who.

**Art.** art/c3-p3-p3.jpg

##### Panel 22

**Picture.** Hagrid points gently toward Harry's forehead, not touching. The scar is in the light. Petunia is a listening silhouette far behind.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks at the scar with reverence and grief. Harry touches it. Petunia is not sneering.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: He tried the Killing Curse on you, and it didn't take. That's why you've got the lightning scar.

**Art.** art/c3-p3-p4.jpg

##### Panel 23

**Picture.** Close on Harry's fingers on the lightning scar. Glasses, hair, the edge of the blanket.

**Faces.** He looks through the moment, remembering a pain he was told was a car crash. Confusion, anger, and the start of grief.

**Caption.** Harry touches his scar.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: This? They told me the crash did it.

**Art.** art/c3-p3-p5.jpg

##### Panel 24

**Picture.** The door is open a crack on a calmer sea. Harry is small in the blanket, the letter in his lap. A mug sits forgotten. The Dursleys are silent shadows.

**Faces.** Hagrid's smile does not quite work. He is proud and sorry. Harry looks at the letter, overwhelmed, not proud.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Every witch and wizard alive knows the name Harry Potter. You're the boy who lived.

**Art.** art/c3-p3-p6.jpg

#### Page 5 — Leaving

##### Panel 25

**Picture.** Dawn is a gray line at the window. Petunia stands, years of spite in her posture. Dudley sleeps on the cot. The knotted rifle is on the floor.

**Faces.** Petunia looks at Harry and sees Lily, ugly with envy. Harry turns as if slapped. Hagrid is angry on Lily's behalf. Vernon looks at the floor.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

**Art.** art/c3-p4-p1.jpg

##### Panel 26

**Picture.** Aunt and nephew, nobody between them. Gray dawn light. The blanket sits on Harry's shoulders. The letter is in his fist.

**Faces.** Harry really looks at her, hurt and curious. Petunia looks just past him. Her mouth is hard.

**Caption.** Harry looks at her as if for the first time.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: You knew what I was. All this time, and you let me sleep in a cupboard.

**Art.** art/c3-p4-p2.jpg

##### Panel 27

**Picture.** The door is open on wet rock and a calmer sea. A motorcycle waits outside. Hagrid's hand is offered. The Dursleys stay small in the dark room.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks sure and kind. Harry looks at the hand, then past it at the sky. Petunia looks at his back.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Come on. We've school things to buy. Robes, books, a wand.

**Art.** art/c3-p4-p3.jpg

##### Panel 28

**Picture.** They stand in the doorway. Inside is brown and small. Outside is silver water and cloud breaking. Hagrid's hand rests on Harry's shoulder.

**Faces.** Hope is naked on Harry's face. Hagrid laughs as if the question is silly and lovely.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

**Art.** art/c3-p4-p4.jpg

##### Panel 29

**Picture.** From inside the hut, looking out. Harry and Hagrid walk onto the rock in the rain. The motorcycle shines. The doorway frames them.

**Faces.** Harry looks back, not waving, not forgiving. Petunia watches, unreadable. Hagrid does not look back.

**Caption.** They step into the rain. Harry looks back once.

**Art.** art/c3-p4-p5.jpg

### Scene — The Leaky Cauldron

#### Page 1 — The street and the pub door

##### Panel 1

**Caption.** A London street in daylight, shops and buses.

##### Panel 2

**Picture.** A cramped London back street in daylight. Bins, pigeons, and a pub squeezed between two shops, painted a little sharper than the street. Muggle pedestrians pass with bags and briefcases.

**Faces.** A woman with a pram looks through Hagrid as if he were a lamppost. Harry looks at her, confused. Hagrid looks at the door, fond.

**Caption.** The Leaky Cauldron, unseen by the street.

##### Panel 3

**Picture.** The door of the Leaky Cauldron, dark wood, a brass handle worn shiny. Hagrid fills it before he opens it. Harry is at his elbow. A warm noise is behind the wood.

**Faces.** Harry hesitates. Hagrid is already home.

**Caption.** The pub door, a step down.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 4

**Picture.** Inside, the pub is low and crowded. Smoke, crooked beams, a bar, witches in pointed hats, a warlock with a newspaper. The room is whole. Nobody has looked at the door yet.

**Faces.** The regulars are mid-drink, mid-argument, ordinary to themselves.

**Caption.** The room, before anyone notices the boy.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 5

**Picture.** A table of old wizards leans together, laughing at a story. A witch feeds something in a cage. Hagrid and Harry are only shapes in the doorway, not yet the center.

**Faces.** The room is busy with its own life. Harry is overwhelmed and has not been seen.

**Caption.** They were already talking over their drinks.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 6

**Picture.** Hagrid's hand is a roof over Harry's shoulder, guiding him one step into the room. Faces are about to turn. They have not. Harry's scar is visible and nobody has said his name.

**Faces.** Harry wants to be smaller. Hagrid looks proud and gentle.

**Caption.** Hagrid steers him in. Still no greeting.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 2 — Inside the pub

##### Panel 7

**Picture.** Inside the Leaky Cauldron. Low beams, a fire, copper pans, smoke. The crowded room freezes mid-drink. Daylight only at the door.

**Faces.** The room looks at Harry with awe. Harry's shoulders are up, overwhelmed. Hagrid looks proud. An old wizard stares at the scar.

**Caption.** Inside, the room goes quiet.

**Dialogue.**

- Old wizard: Bless my soul. Harry Potter. In our pub.

**Art.** art/c4-p1-p2.jpg

##### Panel 8

**Picture.** The pub crowd presses close. Many hands reach in from all sides. One hand is about to touch Harry's hair near the scar. Hagrid's arm is a barrier.

**Faces.** They look at Harry as a story. Harry looks at the floor, shy. Hagrid is polite and ready to move them on.

**Caption.** Strangers reach for his hand. He shrinks.

**Dialogue.**

- Witch: May I shake your hand? The boy who lived.
- Harry: Please. I don't really know what to say.

**Art.** art/c4-p1-p3.jpg

##### Panel 9

**Picture.** The same pub, low beams, fire, copper pans. Hagrid’s moleskin shoulder steers Harry toward a back door, away from the hands. Harry is still in the castoff shirt and jeans, taped glasses, scar on the right brow. The crowd is behind them.

**Faces.** Harry looks smaller after the touching. Hagrid looks protective and ready to leave. The room is no longer reaching.

**Caption.** Hagrid steers him toward the back.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 10

**Picture.** The back door of the pub, still inside. A crack of ordinary daylight, bins hinted beyond. Harry’s hand is not on it yet. The firelight stays on their backs. No alley.

**Faces.** Harry looks at the crack, curious. Hagrid looks sure of the way.

**Caption.** Daylight in the crack of the door.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 11

**Picture.** Harry’s hand on the latch, Hagrid’s huge hand above it. They are still in the pub. The brick wall is not in frame. The room behind them has gone back to its drinks.

**Faces.** Harry is nervous of another room. Hagrid is gentle and impatient.

**Caption.** His hand on the latch. The wall is not open.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 12

**Picture.** They have not gone out. One last look back into the Leaky Cauldron: beams, smoke, a few faces that have already forgotten them. The door is a bright slit.

**Faces.** Harry looks back once, overwhelmed and leaving. Hagrid does not.

**Caption.** The pub lets them go.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

### Scene — The courtyard and the alley

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 3 — The wall opens

##### Panel 13

**Picture.** The courtyard, just outside. Bins, a dripping pipe, the plain brick wall filling the frame. They have come out of the pub door behind them. Daylight, no Diagon Alley yet. Hagrid is huge in the moleskin coat. Harry is small in the castoffs.

**Faces.** Harry looks at a blank wall and does not understand. Hagrid looks at the bricks the way someone looks at a lock.

**Caption.** The courtyard, and a plain wall.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 14

**Picture.** A grimy courtyard behind the pub. Bins, damp stone, a plain brick wall, an ordinary gray cat. Hagrid’s pink umbrella tip touches one brick. His eyes are on that brick. The wall is still a wall. No light in the mortar yet. Harry, in Dudley’s oversized gray shirt and rolled jeans, stands at Hagrid’s elbow.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks at the bricks, concentrating, tongue slightly out. Harry looks at the wall, skeptical and curious. The cat looks at neither.

**Caption.** The umbrella tip touches one brick.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Three up, two across. Watch the bricks.

**Art.** art/c4-p1-p4.jpg

##### Panel 15

**Picture.** The same wall, same daylight. A thin seam of warm light runs along the mortar from the brick the umbrella touched, a few sparks, not a flood. The bricks have not swung. Hagrid’s umbrella is still on the brick. Harry’s eyes follow the seam.

**Faces.** Hagrid watches the mortar, satisfied. Harry looks startled. The cat on the bins watches.

**Caption.** Light runs along the mortar.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 16

**Picture.** The brick wall folds open like a puzzle. A hole widens into light, with a hint of cauldrons, an owl, and a striped awning.

**Faces.** Harry's eyes are huge, fear gone for a second. Hagrid looks at Harry's face, not at the street.

**Caption.** The wall folds open.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c4-p1-p5.jpg

##### Panel 17

**Picture.** Diagon Alley spills out: cobbles, leaning shops, cauldrons, broomsticks, owl cages, and color after the gray street. Harry and Hagrid step through at the bottom.

**Faces.** Harry looks up and around, mouth open, delighted. Hagrid looks ahead, at home. A witch glances at Harry's jeans, amused.

**Caption.** Diagon Alley opens. Harry's eyes go wide.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: What is this place?
- Hagrid: Diagon Alley. Where wizards do their shopping.

**Art.** art/c4-p1-p6.jpg

##### Panel 18

**Picture.** A few steps onto the cobbles. Cauldrons, an owl cage, a striped awning, same daylight. Harry has stopped. Hagrid is a wall of coat behind him. The open brick hole is behind them, not the whole picture. No wand shop yet.

**Faces.** Harry’s eyes are wide and he cannot walk yet. Hagrid looks down, pleased and careful.

**Caption.** He stops. The alley is already busy.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 4 — The white bank

##### Panel 19

**Picture.** The wizard street in daylight, cauldrons and broomsticks in windows, and at the end a snow-white marble bank taller than the shops. Hagrid and Harry walk toward it. Harry's eyes are still too wide from the alley.

**Faces.** Harry is dazzled. Hagrid walks as if the marble were a familiar errand.

**Caption.** Diagon Alley, the white building at the end.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 20

**Picture.** Gringotts' doors, tall, bronze, a goblin in uniform at the side. The steps are white. Wizards queue with keys and nervous faces. Harry looks very small on the marble.

**Faces.** Harry is awed. The goblin guard is bored and sharp.

**Caption.** The bronze doors, from the steps.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 21

**Picture.** The threshold: outside is the alley noise, inside is cool marble and a deeper quiet. The hall opens beyond Hagrid's shoulder, long counters receding.

**Faces.** Harry's mouth is slightly open. Hagrid ducks, out of habit, though the door clears even him.

**Caption.** They pass the doors.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 22

**Picture.** A long marble hall, chandeliers, high counters, goblins in crimson working ledgers. Wizards wait in lines. The scale makes Harry a child again.

**Faces.** Goblins look down because the counters are high, not because they have seen him yet.

**Caption.** The hall, whole, before their key is asked.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 23

**Picture.** One goblin teller, long fingers, counts a stack and ticks a book. A witch collects a receipt and leaves. The next place in line is empty, waiting for Hagrid.

**Faces.** The teller is precise and unimpressed by wizards. He has not looked up.

**Caption.** A teller is already weighing coins.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 24

**Picture.** Hagrid's huge hand on the high counter, a key about to come out of the coat. Harry stands on tiptoe. The teller's eyes are still on the ledger.

**Faces.** Harry is nervous. Hagrid is matter-of-fact. The goblin is not curious yet.

**Caption.** Hagrid sets a hand on the marble. He has not spoken.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 5 — Gringotts

##### Panel 25

**Picture.** Gringotts entrance hall. White marble, chandeliers, long counters, goblins in crimson, wizards queueing. A goblin teller leans over the high counter.

**Faces.** The goblin looks at Harry, sharp and unimpressed. Harry looks up, polite and nervous. Hagrid looks at the goblin, respectful, a key in his palm.

**Caption.** Marble, and a goblin looking down.

**Dialogue.**

- Goblin: Key.
- Hagrid: Vault for Harry Potter. And one more, on the headmaster's order.

**Art.** art/c4-p2-p1.jpg

##### Panel 26

**Picture.** A corridor of bronze vault doors. Cart rails drop into a dark tunnel. A goblin with a lantern walks ahead and does not look back.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks ahead, casual about danger. Harry looks at the dark tunnel, swallowing. The goblin does not look back.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: Goblins are the least of what guards the deep vaults at Gringotts. Dragons, some of them.

**Art.** art/c4-p2-p2.jpg

##### Panel 27

**Picture.** A cart plunges on rails through the caverns. Stalactites, an underground lake, torch brackets. Harry's hair is straight up. Hagrid's beard streams.

**Faces.** Harry looks forward, terrified and thrilled. Hagrid looks at Harry, laughing. The goblin looks at the track, bored.

**Caption.** A cart plunges through the caves.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: Does the cart have to go this fast?
- Hagrid: Best bit of Gringotts, this is.

**Art.** art/c4-p2-p3.jpg

##### Panel 28

**Picture.** A small round vault, door open. Gold, silver, and bronze coins in a heap. The cart waits outside. Harry stands at the edge and does not step in.

**Faces.** Harry looks at the coins, stunned, then at Hagrid for permission. Hagrid nods, soft. The goblin glances at a pocket watch.

**Caption.** Harry's vault. A modest heap of coins.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: This is mine? All of these coins?
- Hagrid: Your mum and dad left them. Galleons, Sickles, Knuts. Take a handful.

**Art.** art/c4-p2-p4.jpg

##### Panel 29

**Picture.** A colder high-security vault. On a stone table, one small parcel wrapped in dirty paper and string. Hagrid's hand closes around it. Harry stays near the door with a pouch of coins.

**Faces.** Hagrid looks at the package, respectful and nervous. Harry looks at it, questions forming. The goblin watches the package.

**Caption.** Hagrid tucks a small package into his coat.

**Dialogue.**

- Hagrid: That's the parcel. Hogwarts business. Don't you touch it.

**Art.** art/c4-p2-p5.jpg

##### Panel 30

**Picture.** The cart climbs toward distant daylight. Hagrid's hand rests over the pocket. The package makes a square shape under the coat. Gold sparks of other vaults far below.

**Faces.** Harry looks at Hagrid, curious, not pushy. Hagrid looks ahead, kind but closed. Harry accepts it with a small frown.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: What's in it?
- Hagrid: Can't say. Dumbledore's orders.

**Art.** art/c4-p2-p6.jpg

**Caption.** Back in the alley, pockets heavier.

**Picture.** Eeylops or the owl window: snowy owls, tawny owls, one snowy owl very still, gold eyes on Harry through the glass. He stops. Hagrid almost walks past, then waits.

**Faces.** The snowy owl looks at Harry as if she already chose. Harry looks back, startled to be chosen.

**Caption.** An owl stares from a shop window.

**Caption.** The shop, mirrors and black cloth.

##### Panel 35

**Picture.** A measuring tape zips around the other student's arms with nobody holding it. Pins hover. The student tries to hold still and fails a little. Harry watches the tape, not the pins.

**Caption.** A tape is already moving for someone else.

**Picture.** Madam Malkin looks over her shoulder at Harry. An empty stool waits. The tape pauses in the air. He has not stepped onto it. Hagrid's bulk is a shadow in the doorway, too big for the shop.

**Caption.** She turns, tape in hand, and sees a new pair of arms.

#### Page 7 — Supplies

##### Panel 37

**Picture.** Madam Malkin's. Mirrors, bolts of black cloth, a window onto the alley. Harry stands on a stool, arms out. A tape measure zips around him with no one holding it. Pins float.

**Faces.** Harry looks down at the tape, delighted and ticklish, a real grin.

**Caption.** A measuring tape moves by itself.

- Madam Malkin: Hold still, dear. Hogwarts robes, is it? Arms out.

##### Panel 38

**Picture.** Flourish and Blotts, crowded, ladders, dust in sunbeams. Harry's hand rests on a heavy book in a stack up to his chin. The cover has a dark, unsmiling face. He does not open it.

**Picture.** The owl shop. Cages wall to wall, feathers on the floor, late light. In the center a large snowy owl, white and gold-eyed, looks only at Harry.

**Caption.** In the window, he does not recognize himself.

**Caption.** The door, a bell that might not ring.

##### Panel 45

**Picture.** The interior, whole: floor to ceiling thin boxes, a ladder, dust, one chair, a counter with a tape lying still. No face. The lamp is the only warmth.

**Picture.** The measuring tape rises off the counter with no hand. Harry has not seen it. Somewhere the silver-haired man is no longer where Harry looked. The next panel is the face, inches away.

**Caption.** A tape lifts on its own, behind him.

### Scene — Outside the wand shop

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 9 — Wand and rival

##### Panel 49

**Picture.** Ollivander's. Thousands of thin boxes, one lamp, dust. Harry turns and Ollivander is inches from his face, pale eyes huge. A measuring tape hangs in the air.

**Faces.** Ollivander looks at the scar, fascinated, not unkind. Harry looks at him, unnerved, polite.

**Caption.** Ollivander is suddenly too close.

**Dialogue.**

- Ollivander: Good afternoon, Mr Potter. I wondered when I should be seeing you.

##### Panel 50

**Picture.** The lamp, the boxes, silence. Ollivander's finger hovers near the scar and does not touch. He holds a slim wand that is not Harry's.

**Faces.** Ollivander looks at the scar, remembering. Harry looks past him, still. He does not blink.

**Caption.** Harry goes still.

**Dialogue.**

- Ollivander: I remember every wand I ever sold. I remember the wand that gave you that scar.

**Art.** art/c4-p4-p2.jpg

##### Panel 51

**Picture.** The shop in disorder. Harry holds a trial wand at arm's length. A vase has shattered. Smoke curls. Boxes tumble. Hagrid's eye is at the window.

**Faces.** Harry looks at the broken vase, guilty. Ollivander looks delighted by the mess. The wand looks wrong in Harry's grip.

**Caption.** Wands reject him. Sparks, smoke, boxes.

**Dialogue.**

**Art.** art/c4-p4-p3.jpg

##### Panel 52

**Picture.** The narrow shop turns beautiful. Gold-white light pours from the wand in Harry's hand, not from anyone's eyes. Ollivander, wispy silver hair, pale silvery eyes, dark old suit, watches the wand. Dust becomes sparks. The lamp is dull beside it.

**Faces.** Harry looks at the wand, wonder, a small smile. Ollivander looks at the pair of them, satisfied and troubled.

**Caption.** One wand stays. Warm light fills the shop.

**Dialogue.**

- Ollivander: Ah. Holly and phoenix feather. Eleven inches. Curious indeed.

##### Panel 53

**Picture.** Quiet shop. A thread of the same wand-light remains. The wand lies between their hands. Ollivander, same dark suit and wispy silver hair, looks at Harry or at the wand, and holds the box in his other hand.

**Faces.** Ollivander looks at Harry, curious and grave. Harry looks at the wand, the smile gone. He understands he is tied to the person who gave him the scar.

**Caption.** None.

**Dialogue.**

- Ollivander: The phoenix gave one other feather, Mr Potter. It sits in the wand that scarred you. You do not choose the wand. The wand does the choosing.

**Art.** art/c4-p4-p5.jpg

**Picture.** Outside the shop, late light. Packages, Hedwig's cage, the wand box under Harry's arm. Draco stands with his chin up, a hand extended. Hagrid is behind Harry with the trunk.

**Faces.** Draco smiles with no warmth and glances at Hagrid's coat with contempt. Harry looks at him, polite and already decided against him, and stays in Hagrid's shadow. Hagrid looks mild, a little sad, not angry.

**Caption.** Harry steps back.

**Dialogue.**

- Draco: That's Hagrid, isn't it. A servant, by the look of the coat.
- Harry: He's a friend.
- Draco: You'll find better company at Hogwarts, if you are the right sort.

## Chapter 5 — The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters

### Scene — King's Cross

One place, one stretch of time. Wardrobe holds for the scene unless a panel shows the change.

#### Page 1 — King's Cross

##### Panel 1

**Picture.** The station exterior, daytime, the brick facade, taxis, a pigeon. A beige car pulls to the curb. Harry is a shape in the back with a trunk too big for him.

**Faces.** The station is busy and ordinary. The car does not belong to a celebration.

**Caption.** King's Cross, the clock and the taxis.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 2

**Picture.** Vernon hauls a heavy trunk onto a trolley and sets a covered cage beside it as if both were rubbish. Harry stands on the pavement in Dudley's old clothes, no robe, no tie. Dudley smirks from the car window.

**Faces.** Vernon is done with this. Dudley is pleased. Harry looks at the station doors.

**Caption.** The boot opens on a cage and a trunk.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 3

**Picture.** Inside the station: the board, the announcement, crowds with ordinary suitcases. Harry pushes the trolley, cage wobbling. No scarlet train. No wizard hats. He is looking for a number that should not exist.

**Faces.** Harry is lost and trying not to show it. Strangers ignore him.

**Caption.** The concourse, a tide of Muggles.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 4

**Picture.** The space between platforms nine and ten. A brick dividing wall. Pigeons. A guard with a thinner mustache than Vernon's, not Vernon, somewhere down the platform, not yet spoken to. Harry stops the trolley and stares at the brick.

**Faces.** Harry looks as if the number has failed him. The barrier looks very solid.

**Caption.** Platforms nine and ten, and a solid brick barrier.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 5

**Picture.** Travelers stream onto platforms nine and ten. Nobody aims at the wall. Harry's hand rests on the trolley. He is about to look for help. He has not asked yet.

**Faces.** He is hopeful and embarrassed already.

**Caption.** People walk through ordinary doors. None walk through brick.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 6

**Picture.** Cut back, brief, to the pavement: Vernon turned in the driver's seat, mouth about to open, Petunia facing forward, Dudley grinning. Harry is on the pavement with the trolley. The line has not been said. The car has not driven off.

**Faces.** Vernon is contemptuous. Harry is braced.

**Caption.** The beige car is still at the curb, in his memory of the last minute.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

#### Page 2 — The barrier

##### Panel 7

**Picture.** Daylight outside the station. A beige car is already pulling away. Harry stands on the pavement with a trolley, a heavy trunk, and a covered cage. A pigeon watches.

**Faces.** Harry looks after the car, hollow, not surprised. Vernon stares ahead and does not wave. Petunia's eyes flick away. Dudley looks at his food.

**Caption.** The Dursleys drive off without a wave.

**Dialogue.**

- Vernon: Platform nine and three-quarters. Load of rubbish. Out you get.

**Art.** art/c5-p1-p1.jpg

##### Panel 8

**Picture.** Under the station roof, before any red-haired family arrives, Harry stands between platforms nine and ten and asks a uniformed platform officer the way. A solid brick barrier fills the gap. A pigeon sits on the bricks.

**Faces.** Harry looks from sign to sign, lost, the hope from Diagon Alley thinning. The commuters do not look at him.

**Caption.** Between platforms 9 and 10. There is no 9¾.

**Dialogue.**

- Harry: Excuse me. Which way is platform nine and three-quarters?

**Art.** art/c5-p1-p2.jpg

##### Panel 9

**Picture.** Beside the barrier, the same platform officer laughs, amused and dismissive, and points Harry away. He does not take him to a train. Hedwig's gold eye shows at the edge of the cloth. No Weasleys yet.

**Faces.** The officer is amused, not cruel, and useless. Harry's ears are red. He is left looking at the bricks, jaw tight. He will not ask again.

**Caption.** The officer laughs and points him away.

**Dialogue.**

- Guard: Platforms nine and ten, lad. There is no nine and three-quarters. Move along.

**Art.** art/c5-p1-p3.jpg

##### Panel 10

**Picture.** A red-haired family cuts through the crowd. Molly turns, already kind. The twins grin at each other. Percy is tidy. Ron pushes a trolley with a rat cage. Ginny holds her mother's coat.

**Faces.** Molly looks at Harry with immediate kindness. Ron sees the scar and goes shy. Harry looks at the family, and hope comes back.

**Caption.** A red-haired family stops with a trolley.

**Dialogue.**

- Molly: Muggles, bless them. First time to Hogwarts? You want the barrier between nine and ten.

**Art.** art/c5-p1-p4.jpg

##### Panel 11

**Picture.** The twins hit the brick at a run and vanish into it, grinning back. Percy follows, neat even while disappearing. The wall looks solid again. Ron hesitates with the rat cage.

**Faces.** The twins are delighted with themselves. Ron looks at the wall, nervous. Molly's hand stays on his back. Harry's mouth is open.

**Caption.** The children run at the barrier and vanish.

**Dialogue.**

- Molly: Fred, George, Percy, straight at the wall, and don't stop. Ron, don't dawdle.

**Art.** art/c5-p1-p5.jpg

##### Panel 12

**Picture.** Harry shuts his eyes and runs. The trolley charges. The brick swallows the front of the trunk. His jacket flaps. Hedwig's cage rocks, and her eye stays calm.

**Faces.** His eyes are shut, so the feeling is in his mouth: braced, brave, a little sick. Molly's hand lifts behind him, out of focus.

**Caption.** Harry shuts his eyes and runs. The wall takes him.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** art/c5-p1-p6.jpg

**Dialogue.** None.

##### Panel 14

**Picture.** Platform nine and three-quarters in one wide picture. The Hogwarts Express is scarlet and huge. Steam rolls under the roof. The scarlet is the train, not a robe.

**Caption.** The platform, whole, and a scarlet engine.

##### Panel 15

**Picture.** Students in mixed travel clothes, not house robes, hug parents. An owl hoots in a cage. A cat glares from a basket. A red-haired woman who is not yet a conversation is pinning a name to a trunk far down the platform.

**Caption.** Families were already saying goodbye.

##### Panel 16

##### Panel 17

**Picture.** Through a carriage window: an empty compartment, seats, a small table. Harry sees it. Other compartments are full of owls and elbows.

**Faces.** He looks relieved and shy.

**Caption.** An empty compartment waits.

**Dialogue.** None.

**Art.** Not drawn yet.

##### Panel 18

**Picture.** Down the platform, the conductor raises a hand as if the shout is next. Harry grips the trolley. The train breathes. No line yet.
